# Claim fidelity

Generated by `measure/fidelity.py`. A diagnostic, not an experiment.

- Papers asked: **113**
- Answered and graded: **98**
- Showed real knowledge of the paper (`recalled`): **1** — 1%
- Claims scored: **1231**
- Claims the answer invented: **419**

Answered by `meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct`, graded by `meta-llama/llama-3-3-70b-instruct`.

- **2 — claim and scope correct:** 0 (0%)
- **1 — claim correct, scope dropped:** 20 (2%)  ← the cell sidecars exist to move
- **0 — claim wrong or misattributed:** 1211 (98%)

## This is a floor, not a worklist

97 of 98 papers were not recognised at all, and the answers are not blank -- they are
fluent, specific and about papers that do not exist: 419 invented claims against
1231 authored ones. So the per-claim table below ranks nothing, because every paper
scored the same score. What the run establishes is a baseline -- this engine cannot
describe this corpus unaided -- which is the gap the pages exist to close.

For a ranking instead, ask an engine that retrieves. Answers have to be able to differ
before their scores can.

## Before trusting these numbers

Hand-check a stratified 20% of the scores. The grader is the measurement
instrument and needs its own validation; an unvalidated grader produces
a confident report about nothing.
